### 🎓 [The Explainer Series: Supermarkets](https://www.slq.qld.gov.au/whats-on/explainer-series-supermarkets)
📆 Thu 24 Sep, 6:30 PM  
📍 State Library of Queensland, Stanley Place, South Bank  
💰 See link  
`history` `technology` `shopping` `culture`

The Explainer Series is about exploring the questions we’re all asking.Supermarkets are part of everyday life, but there’s a lot more happening in the aisles than deciding what’s for dinner. From pricing and loyalty programs to store layouts, online shopping and the growing influence of AI, the way we buy our groceries is changing fast. The Explainer Series: Supermarkets will take a look behind the shelves to explore how Australian supermarkets became the giants they are today - and where they might be heading next. As smaller stores, home delivery, automated fulfillment and personalised shopping reshape the supermarket experience, what do we gain from all that convenience, and what might we be giving up? Join us as we unpack the power and psychology of supermarkets, the technology changing the way we shop, and what the supermarket of the future might look like.

### 📌 [Karawatha Forest BioBlitz](https://events.griffith.edu.au/EARQlD)
📆 Sat 26 Sep, 7:00 AM  
📍 Karawatha Forest Park and Discovery Centre, Karawatha Forest Park and Discovery Centre, 149 Acacia Road, Karawatha  
💰 Free  
`nature` `science` `technology` `family` `free`

Join us for a fun and fascinating BioBlitz at Karawatha Forest, where nature enthusiasts of all ages can help discover and record local wildlife. Spend the day exploring this beautiful bushland in Karawatha, using the free iNaturalist app to identify fungi, plants, insects, birds and more. Your observations will contribute valuable data to real biodiversity research. Whether you’re a seasoned naturalist or just curious about your local environment, this hands-on citizen science event is a great way for the whole family to connect with nature, don’t miss it! Requirements on the day: Arrive 10 minutes before starting time to check in with staff at the Environment Centre. All children must be accompanied and directly supervised by an adult. Wear sun-safe clothing, fully enclosed shoes, and bring sunscreen. Bring water and a snack if required as there are no food outlets on site. Note: This event will go ahead in light rain. Events are subject to weather and environmental conditions and may be cancelled on the day. Enquiries: For enquiries about this event, please contact Dr Wade Hadwen from Griffith University by phoning 07 3735 3987 or email . Meeting point: Karawatha Forest Discovery Centre. Recommended: Download the app "iNaturalist" before attending.

#### [Make Believe: Magic, the Worlds of the Jim Henson Company](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/make-believe-magic-the-worlds-of-the-jim-henson-company)
📆 Sat 12 Sep 2026, 10:00 AM – Sun 18 Apr 2027
📍 GOMA, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Buy Tickets
`art` `exhibition` `gallery` `film` `design` `performance`

Celebrating 70 years of creativity and innovation, the exclusive-to-Brisbane exhibition ‘Make, Believe, Magic: The Worlds of The Jim Henson Company’ will be an unprecedented exploration of world-building and imaginative storytelling. Founded by Jim and Jane Henson in 1955, The Jim Henson Company has created some of the most cherished characters in screen history and continues to revolutionise the art of puppetry for television and film. Their indelible imprint on entertainment, education and popular culture globally is seen in the much-loved series Fraggle Rock and Fraggle Rock: Back to the Rock, the treasured fantasy worlds of The Dark Crystal and Labyrinth, the iconic characters of the Muppets, and the instantly recognisable residents of Sesame Street. In a world-first, ‘Make, Believe, Magic: The Worlds of The Jim Henson Company’ will include the Great Hall set from the Emmy Award-winning television series Fraggle Rock: Back to the Rock, taking audiences behind the scenes of a working puppeteer’s film set. Immense and immersive, the exhibition will explore the Company’s dynamic trajectory from practical puppetry to today’s animatronic and digital innovations, as well as its ongoing dedication to the craft of creature design and trailblazing developments in on-screen puppeteering. It will feature hundreds of objects, from practical puppets and animatronic creatures to props, costumes, original drawings and moving image clips. Screening alongside the exhibition throughout its run will be three, free film programs at GOMA’s Australian Cinémathèque as well as a suite of QAGOMA’s renowned public, learning and access programs inviting audiences to explore and engage with the exhibition themes and artworks. This exhibition is organised by the Queensland Art Gallery | Gallery of Modern Art in collaboration with The Jim Henson Company. QAGOMA is excited to be celebrating the opening of ‘Make, Believe, Magic: The Worlds of The Jim Henson Company’ in association with Brisbane Festival in September. Works in this exhibition are protected under the Australian Government’s Protection of Cultural Objects on Loan Act 2013. Find out more.

#### [The God of Small Things: Faith and Popular Culture](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/the-god-of-small-things-faith-and-popular-culture)
📆 Sat 20 Sep 2025, 10:00 AM – Mon 5 Oct 2026
📍 QAG, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`faith` `culture` `art` `history` `free`

'The God of Small Things: Faith and Popular Culture' draws its title from Arundhati Roy's evocative novel to explore the omnipresence of faith in the mundane and extraordinary alike. Centred around a rare collection of embellished oleographs by Raja Ravi Varma (India, 1848-1906), the exhibition delves into the intersection between devotional imagery and popular culture, capturing the divine as a living part of everyday life. Works from the QAGOMA contemporary Asian collection complement and extend this dialogue, through the juxtaposition of religious and vernacular iconographies with contemporary reflections on belief. QAGOMA has embarked on a landmark conservation and research exchange with colleagues in India to document and conserve a rare collection of Ravi Varma works. Specialists from QAGOMA’s conservation team will collaborate with the Museum of Art and Photography (MAP), Bengaluru, to share expertise and deepen cultural ties. QAGOMA’s conservation and research exchange is supported by the Centre for Australia-India Relations. QAGOMA is proud to be a recipient of a Centre for Australia-India Relations Maitri grant.

#### [Sorcerers from Bohemia: Picasso’s Saltimbanques](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/sorcerers-from-bohemia-picassos-saltimbanques)
📆 Sat 14 Feb 2026, 10:00 AM – Sun 16 Jan 2028
📍 QAG, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`picasso` `printmaking` `art` `history` `free`

'Sorcerers from Bohemia' evokes the free-spirited world of the travelling circus performers, or saltimbanques — the subject of Pablo Picasso’s first major body of work in printmaking. 'La Suite des Saltimbanques' is a series of 15 loosely related etchings and drypoints that mark a transitional moment in the young artist’s personal and artistic development. Created on the cusp of Picasso’s Blue (1901–04) and Rose (1904–06) periods, the works are intimately connected with his paintings and drawings of the same era, including the Gallery’s own La Belle Hollandaise of 1905. On moving to Paris in 1904, 22-year-old Picasso encountered a bohemian crowd of acrobats, jugglers and street performers, whom he sketched as they rehearsed and socialised together. His early depictions focus on the vulnerability and poverty of these itinerant figures, whereas later works pay homage to their strong social bonds and the freedom of their creative lives. Appearing individually and in groups, Picasso’s saltimbanques vary widely in age and fulfil a variety of roles, from clown and friend to mother and king. Ushering in a lifelong experimentation with printmaking, 'La Suite des Saltimbanques' offers extraordinary insights into Picasso’s formative artistic concerns and the social milieu he inhabited at the time. In these sensitive and sympathetic portraits, he conveys the heft of a body, the texture of hair, a playful pose or a tender gesture with remarkable economy and elegance of line, ennobling figures who ordinarily existed on the fringes of society.

#### [Towards a Collapsing World: German Expressionism](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/towards-a-collapsing-world-german-expressionism)
📆 Sat 14 Feb 2026, 10:00 AM – Sun 16 Jan 2028
📍 QAG, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`expressionism` `art` `history` `printmaking` `free`

The early decades of the twentieth century witnessed an extraordinary moment in German art, as artists responded to a modern world in flux with works that engaged deeply with contemporary society, politics and culture. ‘Towards a Collapsing World’ explores this period of significant cultural flourishing through a survey of works on paper by leading figures of German Expressionism. Rather than a single, unified approach, German Expressionism was characterised by a plurality of attitudes and styles, with artist groups such as Die Brücke (The Bridge) and Der Blaue Reiter (The Blue Rider) forming and dissolving in response to fluctuating ideologies. Common to these disparate voices was a shared belief in the power of art to effect social change. The woodcut print became an important vehicle within German Expressionism, reviving a rich medieval heritage. Its stark, simplified forms — expressed through bold, flat patterns and raw aesthetic intensity —conveyed the inner turmoil and social critique of these expressionist artists with impassioned urgency.

#### [Botanic By Night: NightGlow](https://botanicbynight.com.au/)
📆 Mon 28 Sep, 6:30 PM
📍 Brisbane Botanic Gardens Mt Coot-tha, Brisbane Botanic Gardens Mt Coot-tha, 152 Mt Coot-tha Road, Toowong
💰 See website
`art` `performance` `nature` `family`

Botanic By Night: NightGlow makes its Brisbane debut at Brisbane Botanic Gardens Mt Coot-tha from 25 September to 10 October, transforming the Gardens into a magical after-dark outdoor art experience. Visitors will journey along an enchanting 800-metre route featuring 11 larger-than-life illuminated animal sculptures inspired by Queensland's native wildlife. Designed by Ample Projects in collaboration with First Nations artists Casey Coolwell-Fisher and Nathan Peckham, Botanic By Night: NightGlow combines art, storytelling and sound to celebrate the animals, habitats and stories that shape Queensland's natural environment. From kangaroos and koalas to platypus, dingoes and green tree frogs, each artwork offers a unique encounter, inviting visitors to wander, explore and connect with nature in a whole new way. Perfect for families, friends and date nights, Botanic By Night: NightGlow also features a dedicated food truck precinct.

`art` `exhibition` `history` `design` `culture` `free`

What makes a collection? Museum of Brisbane is giving you the keys to some of the most awe-inspiring collections across our amazing city. An irresistible array of breathtaking curiosities, Precious brings together thousands of remarkable items from more than 30 public and private collections. From textiles to tin-toys, micro-architectural marvels to marine treasures, this exhibition is a joyful celebration of the art of assemblage. We invite you to immerse yourself into the incredible stories of some of the prolific collectors of Brisbane, and get to know what makes a keepsake and collectible truly Precious. Open daily from 10am - 5pm. Museum of Brisbane is Brisbane City Council's leading history and art museum where you can experience our city's vibrant culture.

#### [Great and Small](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/great-and-small)
📆 Sat 21 Jun 2025, 10:00 AM – Sun 23 May 2027
📍 QAG, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`animals` `indigenous` `culture` `art` `free`

Animals have played a central part in Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander culture and spiritual beliefs for over 60,000 years. Native fauna form an integral part of relationships with Country, in community and are a foundation of economies, identity and sustainability. Over many millennia of co-existence animals have been central to survival as a food source, for First Nations people they are also absorbed into lore and culture as images of totemic power; and into the ancestral creation stories that link people, land and animals. ‘Great and small’ is a celebration of these connections.

#### [Inscribing a Life](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/inscribing-a-life)
📆 Sat 27 Sep 2025, 10:00 AM – Sun 18 Oct 2026
📍 GOMA, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`art` `history` `memorial` `collection` `free`

‘Inscribing a life’ brings artworks from across the QAGOMA Collection that register existence, histories, and time through the act of mark making. From the brute force recorded in paint on a canvas that expresses strength and vitality, to the time commitment captured in the repetition involved in making intricate forms, the desire to record and share expressions of life is championed in this exhibition. Complementing the adjacent display of ‘Archie Moore: kith and kin’ both presentations share the themes of memorials and cosmic time. Alongside artworks expressing the intensity and wonder of existence are those that celebrate and solemnly honour lives that have come to an end. These artworks speak to the importance of acknowledging loss and how through observing death the public memorial reaffirms the social fabric for the living. The importance of thinking beyond human lifespan can be seen in expressions of the cosmos, 'Inscribing a life' features artworks that grapple with the vast scale and the deep time of the universe.

#### [Outdoor Gallery: Frazer Campbell exhibition ‘Portraiture in the Square’ at Town Square, Fish Lane Arts Precinct](https://www.brisbane.qld.gov.au/events-arts-and-culture/art-and-culture/outdoor-gallery-and-public-art)
📆 Wed 5 Aug – Mon 12 Oct
📍 Brisbane City Council What's On
💰 Free
`art` `exhibition` `performance` `free`

‘Portrait in the Square’ by Frazer Campbell is a pop-up exhibition presented in partnership between Brisbane City Council’s Outdoor Gallery and Fish Lane Arts Precinct. It will be on display from 5 August to 11 October 2026. Frazer Campbell’s portraiture, installed in the vitrines at Fish Lane’s Town Square, offers a contemplative pause within one of Brisbane’s busiest urban corridors. The works draw out the layered depth of individual lives in a fast-paced city environment. These original artworks act as quiet anchors, reflecting personal histories and the universal human experience and inviting passersby to recognise fragments of their own stories in another’s gaze, gesture or posture. Technically, Frazer's practice sits at the intersection of close observation and expressive mark-making, using layered surfaces and tonal nuance to suggest the complexity of identity. Frazer Campbell is a Brisbane-based emerging artist known for his intense, emotionally charged portraits in oils and acrylics. Influenced by literature, film and music, his award-winning work explores identity and connection through colour, cinematic drama, and deep empathy, all shaped by his lived experience. “I am intrigued by certain faces, especially expressions at the unhappier end of the emotional spectrum: existential angst, psychic dread.” — Frazer Campbell Frazer Campbell is featured in Council’s latest Outdoor Gallery exhibition, AMPLIFY ME! , a celebration of Brisbane artists with lived experience of disability that showcases their work in accessible outdoor spaces across the city. Grounded in inclusive arts practice, the exhibition encourages collaboration and barrier-free creativity, supporting lasting connections, inclusion and social change across Brisbane’s cultural landscape. AMPLIFY ME! is curated by Nicole Crosswell and runs from 21 November 2025 to 25 October 2026. This program is part of Council’s Outdoor Gallery, which transforms Brisbane’s laneways, streets and car parks into imaginative spaces for everyone, showcasing art in light boxes, banners, vitrines and evening projections. This program is delivered in partnership with Fish Lane Arts Precinct. Image Credit: Photography by Joseph Rucklie. Location: The vitrines at Fish Lane’s Town Square.

#### [Beyond Henson: Puppetry and World Cinema](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/cinema/program/beyond-henson-puppetry-and-world-cinema)
📆 Sat 12 Sep 2026, 12:01 AM – Sun 18 Apr 2027
📍 GOMA, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`film` `puppetry` `cinema` `history` `free`

Inspired by the work of The Jim Henson Company and Jim Henson’s Creature Shop, the film program ‘Beyond Henson: Puppetry and World Cinema’ surveys the rich international history of storytelling through the artforms of cinema and puppetry. Presented in conjunction with the ‘Make, Believe, Magic: The Worlds of The Jim Henson Company’ exhibition (12 September 2026 – 18 April 2027), the film program delves into crafted narratives that harness traditions of puppetry and cinema to move beyond belief and into the realms of the imagination. To trace the anatomy of onscreen puppetry is to draw from millennia of performance and craft traditions that extend across the globe. From the marionette puppetry of France and the Bunraku theatre of Japan, to the Wayang Kulit puppetry of Indonesia and the British Punch and Judy show, the democratised nature of the puppet theatre has enabled an unbridled style of performance that can enact the truthful and the taboo. Taking direction from the television series Jim Henson Presents the World of Puppetry 1985, the selected titles include films that precede (and sit adjacent to) productions by The Jim Henson Company, positioning the Company’s work within the broader canon of on-screen puppetry. [sponsorLogos block] QAGOMA acknowledges the generous assistance of the National Film and Sound Archive of Australia, Canberra; the Academy Film Archive, Los Angeles; the British Film Institute, London; the NBCUniversal Archives & Collections, Los Angeles; the Library of Congress, Washington; the National Film Archive, Prague, the Australian Film Television and Radio School, Sydney and the Revival House, Perth in providing materials for this program. Program curated by Victoria Wareham, Australian Cinémathèque with additional film note writing by Rebekah Brammer.

#### [Where the Wild Things Are Made: Jim Henson’s Creature Shop](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/cinema/program/where-the-wild-things-are-made-jim-hensons-creature-shop)
📆 Sat 12 Sep 2026, 12:01 AM – Sun 18 Apr 2027
📍 GOMA, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`film` `creatures` `craft` `cinema` `free`

Presented alongside the ‘Make, Believe, Magic: The Worlds of The Jim Henson Company’ special exhibition (12 September 2026 – 18 April 2027), ‘Where the Wild Things Are Made: Jim Henson’s Creature Shop’ celebrates the skill and craftsmanship of the artisans at Jim Henson’s Creature Shop. Highlighting the diverse portfolio of expressive characters and creatures made by the Company for external productions, the selected films showcase the work of Jim Henson’s Creature Shop and how it continues to shape the landscape of cinematic storytelling. An important facet of The Jim Henson Company, Jim Henson’s Creature Shop is internationally renowned for creating pioneering techniques that bring extraordinary characters and stories to life. Formally established following the release of Labyrinth in 1986, Jim Henson’s Creature Shop continues to be an enduring creative hub where performance and craftsmanship is paired with experimentation and ground-breaking technical innovation to transform the imaginary into reality. [sponsorLogos block] QAGOMA acknowledges the generous assistance of the National Film and Sound Archive of Australia, Canberra; the Norwegian Film Institute, Oslo and the Revival House, Perth in providing materials for this program. Program curated by Victoria Wareham, Australian Cinémathèque with additional film note writing by Rebekah Brammer.

#### [Geoffrey Burns: Ride the Chronovore](https://www.parkercontemporary.com.au/exhibitions/geoffrey-burns)
📆 Wed 16 Sep, 12:00 PM – Sat 3 Oct
📍 PARKER Contemporary, 38 Hope Street
💰 See link
`contemporary art` `painting` `exhibition` `gallery` `art`

Revisiting locations at different times of year, Geoffrey Burns approaches making as both a method and a measure of time. Works emerge through repeated encounters with place, where observation accumulates and memory takes form through surface and seasonal change. Ride the Chronovore considers time as an active force: consuming, transforming and leaving its evidence behind. Through repetition, Burns traces how surfaces shift, forms appear and recede, and environments register cycles that often unfold beyond human attention. Each return gathers new information while revealing what has altered, disappeared or endured. The fleeting span of a human life meets the slower tempo of the natural world, inviting an attentive and materially grounded way of looking at the quiet force of time. Opening Night Friday September 18 6 - 8pm VIP Preview from 5.30pm Geoffrey Burns in Conversation Sunday September 20 3pm - 4pm + celebratory drinks with the artist until 5pm Artist in Residence Saturday September 26 12pm - 4pm (drop in any time) Morning Musings Workshop Retreat Sunday October 4 10am - 12pm

Celebrating Country and Culture through the bold designs of Magpie Goose. Museum of Brisbane is excited to collaborate with Magpie Goose, a proudly Aboriginal owned and led fashion and social enterprise, to celebrate extraordinary First Nations cultural stories and designs. This exclusive to Brisbane exhibition, developed in close collaboration with Magpie Goose owners Amanda Hayman (Wakka Wakka and Kalkadoon) and Troy Casey (Kamilaroi), offers a deep dive into the work of some of the region’s most talented First Nations artists and designers. Using clothing as a canvas for storytelling, this exciting collaboration will connect audiences with the rich and diverse cultural expressions of First Nations communities in Queensland and beyond. Discover the artists and stories behind the brand that’s making a difference, one garment at a time. Stories You Wear: Magpie Goose is supported by the Queensland Government through Arts Queensland. Museum of Brisbane is Brisbane City Council’s leading history and art museum where you can experience our city’s vibrant culture. For more information, visit MoB website. #### [Artist in Residence: Renee Kire](https://www.museumofbrisbane.com.au/whats-on/renee-kire/)
📆 Thu 24 Sep, 10:00 AM
📍 Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City, Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City Hall, 64 Adelaide Street, Brisbane City
💰 Free
`art` `exhibition` `design` `sculpture` `performance` `free`

Step inside an exciting new residency project that invites you to experience art in unexpected ways. Artist in Residence Renee Kire takes over Museum of Brisbane’s entryway, transforming these spaces with a vibrant and dynamic mix of striking vinyl graphics and sculptural timber elements. These features create an immersive pathway that stretches across walls and ceilings, guiding you into the heart of the Museum. Drawing inspiration from Minimalism while reinterpreting the movement through a contemporary feminist lens, Kire brings fresh attention to the often-overlooked contributions of women in the field. Bright, colourful and welcoming for all visitors, the artwork is both joyful and thought-provoking. Responding directly to the architecture of the Museum, City Hall and the Brisbane cityscape, Kire’s design reveals unexpected connections between the built environment and abstract form. Repetition, rhythm and thoughtful details invite you to slow down, look closely and discover new perspectives. MoB’s Artist in Residence program is supported by Tim Fairfax AC. Museum of Brisbane is Brisbane City Council’s leading history and art museum where you can experience our city’s vibrant culture.

#### [Warrajamba](https://www.museumofbrisbane.com.au/whats-on/warrajamba/)
📆 Thu 24 Sep, 10:00 AM
📍 Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City, Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City Hall, 64 Adelaide Street, Brisbane City
💰 Free
`art` `sculpture` `indigenous` `history` `culture` `free`

Follow the mermaid in the bay. Dive into story, culture and hands-on creativity with our latest Artist in Residence, Quandamooka artist Delvene Cockatoo-Collins. Journey alongside Delvene to the shores of Minjerribah and discover the ancestral story of Warrajamba, the mermaid, a living cultural legacy that Delvene continues to explore as she searches for its lost ending. Get hands-on and create your own mermaid or dugong using artist-designed stamps, connecting with the stories of Warrajamba and Yungong, the dugong. Discover Delvene’s powerful sculptural work made from materials gathered from Country - yungair/yungaire (freshwater reed), talwalpin (beach hibiscus cotton tree), and quampie shell. Come and be immersed in a space that celebrates care for land, sea and story. MoB’s Artist in Residence program is supported by Tim Fairfax AC. The Creative Space is proudly supported by Major Partner Brisbane Airport and generously supported by Ian and Cass George, and Holly and John Livingstone. Warrajamba is supported by the Queensland Government through Arts Queensland. Museum of Brisbane is Brisbane City Council’s leading history and art museum where you can experience our city’s vibrant culture.

`art` `exhibition` `painting` `sculpture` `drawing` `performance`

Brisbane-based figurative artist Scott Breton combines classical training with a uniquely contemporary vision to explore the living presence of figure, form and space in his work. With a practice informed by deep study of traditional drawing, painting and sculptural media, Breton’s compositions often convey a sense of aliveness and psychological depth, where gesture and structure meet imagination and atmosphere. A recipient of Australia’s prestigious A.M.E. Bale Travelling Scholarship, his work bridges the enduring principles of representational art with fresh insights into the human experience. Breton’s upcoming exhibition offers a new chapter in his ongoing investigation of form, psyche and the poetic potential of the painted surface.

#### [Warrajamba](https://www.museumofbrisbane.com.au/whats-on/warrajamba/)
📆 Fri 25 Sep, 10:00 AM
📍 Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City, Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City Hall, 64 Adelaide Street, Brisbane City
💰 Free
`art` `sculpture` `indigenous` `history` `culture` `free`

Follow the mermaid in the bay. Dive into story, culture and hands-on creativity with our latest Artist in Residence, Quandamooka artist Delvene Cockatoo-Collins. Journey alongside Delvene to the shores of Minjerribah and discover the ancestral story of Warrajamba, the mermaid, a living cultural legacy that Delvene continues to explore as she searches for its lost ending. Get hands-on and create your own mermaid or dugong using artist-designed stamps, connecting with the stories of Warrajamba and Yungong, the dugong. Discover Delvene’s powerful sculptural work made from materials gathered from Country - yungair/yungaire (freshwater reed), talwalpin (beach hibiscus cotton tree), and quampie shell. Come and be immersed in a space that celebrates care for land, sea and story. MoB’s Artist in Residence program is supported by Tim Fairfax AC. The Creative Space is proudly supported by Major Partner Brisbane Airport and generously supported by Ian and Cass George, and Holly and John Livingstone. Warrajamba is supported by the Queensland Government through Arts Queensland. Museum of Brisbane is Brisbane City Council’s leading history and art museum where you can experience our city’s vibrant culture.

#### [Botanic By Night: NightGlow](https://botanicbynight.com.au/)
📆 Fri 25 Sep, 6:30 PM
📍 Brisbane Botanic Gardens Mt Coot-tha, Brisbane Botanic Gardens Mt Coot-tha, 152 Mt Coot-tha Road, Toowong
💰 See website
`art` `nature` `wildlife` `family`

Botanic By Night: NightGlow makes its Brisbane debut at Brisbane Botanic Gardens Mt Coot-tha from 25 September to 10 October, transforming the Gardens into a magical after-dark outdoor art experience. Visitors will journey along an enchanting 800-metre route featuring 11 larger-than-life illuminated animal sculptures inspired by Queensland's native wildlife. Designed by Ample Projects in collaboration with First Nations artists Casey Coolwell-Fisher and Nathan Peckham, Botanic By Night: NightGlow combines art, storytelling and sound to celebrate the animals, habitats and stories that shape Queensland's natural environment. From kangaroos and koalas to platypus, dingoes and green tree frogs, each artwork offers a unique encounter, inviting visitors to wander, explore and connect with nature in a whole new way. Perfect for families, friends and date nights, Botanic By Night: NightGlow also features a dedicated food truck precinct.

#### [Fallen Angels (1995)](https://www.fivestarcinemas.com.au/new-farm/movie/fallen-angels)
📆 Fri 25 Sep, 7:00 PM
📍 New Farm Cinemas, 701 Brunswick St, New Farm
💰 See link
`film` `performance`

Classic screening. New Farm Cinemas: Fri 25 September Lost souls reach out for human connection amidst the glimmering night world of Hong Kong in Wong Kar Wai’s hallucinatory, neon-soaked nocturne. This hyper-cool head rush plays like the dark, moody flip side to Wong’s breakout feature 'Chungking Express' as it charts the subtly interlacing fates of a handful of urban loners, including a coolly detached hitman (Leon Lai) looking to go straight, his business partner (Michelle Reis) who secretly yearns for him, and a mute delinquent (Takeshi Kaneshiro) who wreaks mischief by night. Swinging between hardboiled noir and slapstick lunacy with giddy abandon, Fallen Angels is both a dizzying, dazzling city symphony and a poignant meditation on love, loss, and longing in a metropolis that never sleeps.

#### [35mm The Blair Witch Project (1999)](https://www.fivestarcinemas.com.au/new-farm/movie/35mm-the-blair-witch-project)
📆 Fri 25 Sep, 7:00 PM
📍 New Farm Cinemas, 701 Brunswick St, New Farm
💰 See link
`film` `performance`

35mm Film · Classic screening. REAL FILM. REAL CINEMA. At Tower Cinemas, we keep 35mm film alive. In a world of digital projection and shrinking formats, celluloid is becoming rare, and we think that matters. We hunt down original 35mm prints, often unseen for years, and put them back where they belong: on the big screen. This is cinema with depth, texture, and impact. The way it was meant to be experienced. WHY 35MM? Digital runs at 2K or 4K. 35mm? Closer to 8K. That means sharper detail, richer contrast, and deeper, more natural colour. Film simply looks and feels different—more alive. And it’s not just the format—it’s the craft. Every session is run by a real projectionist, presenting the film with care and precision. Sure, digital is consistent. Film isn’t—and that’s the point. Every print carries a history. Slight imperfections, subtle wear—that’s character. That’s warmth. That’s cinema. Three film students travel to Maryland to make a student film about a local urban legend... The Blair Witch. The three went into the woods on a two day hike to find the Blair Witch, and never came back. One year later, the students film and video were found in the woods. The footage was compiled and made into a movie. The Blair Witch Project.

#### [The Room](https://www.fivestarcinemas.com.au/new-farm/movie/the-room)
📆 Fri 25 Sep, 9:00 PM
📍 New Farm Cinemas, 701 Brunswick St, New Farm
💰 See link
`film` `performance`

Classic screening. Last Friday of the month. Written, directed and starring Tommy Wiseau, The Room is like no other film you’ve seen. Johnny (Tommy Wiseau) seems to have it all- a beautiful fiancée, Lisa, great friends and a job with a promotion in the works. But Lisa, (Juliette Danielle) isn’t who she seems. Manipulative and restless, Lisa sets her sights on Johnny's best friend Mark (Greg Sestero) and they start a torrid affair and Johnny’s perfect world starts to crumble. Featured on Adult Swim’s Tim and Eric, Awesome Show, Great Job! The Room has garnered a massive cult following, selling out sessions across the US, leaving audiences stunned and wondering “how can so bad, be so good?” Bring your plastic spoons!

#### [Stories You Wear: Magpie Goose](https://www.museumofbrisbane.com.au/whats-on/stories-you-wear/)
📆 Sat 26 Sep
📍 Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City, Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City Hall, 64 Adelaide Street, Brisbane City
💰 Free
`art` `exhibition` `fashion` `indigenous` `culture` `history`

Celebrating Country and Culture through the bold designs of Magpie Goose. Museum of Brisbane is excited to collaborate with Magpie Goose, a proudly Aboriginal owned and led fashion and social enterprise, to celebrate extraordinary First Nations cultural stories and designs. This exclusive to Brisbane exhibition, developed in close collaboration with Magpie Goose owners Amanda Hayman (Wakka Wakka and Kalkadoon) and Troy Casey (Kamilaroi), offers a deep dive into the work of some of the region’s most talented First Nations artists and designers. Using clothing as a canvas for storytelling, this exciting collaboration will connect audiences with the rich and diverse cultural expressions of First Nations communities in Queensland and beyond. Discover the artists and stories behind the brand that’s making a difference, one garment at a time. Stories You Wear: Magpie Goose is supported by the Queensland Government through Arts Queensland. Museum of Brisbane is Brisbane City Council’s leading history and art museum where you can experience our city’s vibrant culture. For more information, visit MoB website. Image: Magpie Goose x Quandamooka Artists, by Amanda Hayman & Troy Casey, Country to Couture, 2025, Photo by Dylan Buckee.

#### [Artist in Residence: Renee Kire](https://www.museumofbrisbane.com.au/whats-on/renee-kire/)
📆 Sat 26 Sep, 10:00 AM
📍 Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City, Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City Hall, 64 Adelaide Street, Brisbane City
💰 Free
`art` `exhibition` `design` `sculpture` `performance` `free`

Step inside an exciting new residency project that invites you to experience art in unexpected ways. Artist in Residence Renee Kire takes over Museum of Brisbane’s entryway, transforming these spaces with a vibrant and dynamic mix of striking vinyl graphics and sculptural timber elements. These features create an immersive pathway that stretches across walls and ceilings, guiding you into the heart of the Museum. Drawing inspiration from Minimalism while reinterpreting the movement through a contemporary feminist lens, Kire brings fresh attention to the often-overlooked contributions of women in the field. Bright, colourful and welcoming for all visitors, the artwork is both joyful and thought-provoking. Responding directly to the architecture of the Museum, City Hall and the Brisbane cityscape, Kire’s design reveals unexpected connections between the built environment and abstract form. Repetition, rhythm and thoughtful details invite you to slow down, look closely and discover new perspectives. MoB’s Artist in Residence program is supported by Tim Fairfax AC. Museum of Brisbane is Brisbane City Council’s leading history and art museum where you can experience our city’s vibrant culture.

Brisbane through the lens of local photographers. The winners exhibition of the 2026 Lord Mayor’s Photographic Awards brings together striking photographic works that capture the city’s spirit - from familiar streets and riverside scenes to the fleeting moments, routines and characters that make Brisbane feel like home. New this year, the Brisbane Postcards Collection category invites photographers to showcase Brisbane’s charm and energy through images that locals recognise, and visitors instantly fall in love with. Selected winning works will be available as collectible postcards at MoB Shop. Open to anyone who lives, works or studies in Brisbane, the 2026 Awards celebrate what makes our city unique through this year’s theme, Everyday Brisbane. For more information, visit our website . Museum of Brisbane is Brisbane City Council’s leading history and art museum where you can experience our city’s vibrant culture. For more information, please visit MoB website .

#### [Chinatown (1974)](https://www.fivestarcinemas.com.au/new-farm/movie/chinatown)
📆 Sun 27 Sep, 2:10 PM
📍 New Farm Cinemas, 701 Brunswick St, New Farm
💰 See link
`film` `performance`

Classic screening. Regal Graceville: Sat 26 September. New Farm Cinemas: Sun 27 September. In this seminal noir from 1975, Jack Nicholson is unforgettable as private eye Jake Gittes, living off the murky moral climate of sunbaked pre-war Southern California. Hired by a beautiful socialite (Faye Dunaway) to investigate her husband’s extra-marital affair, Gittes is swept into a maelstrom of double dealings and deadly deceits as he uncovers a web of personal and political scandals that come crashing together in one unforgettable night.

#### [Botanic By Night: NightGlow](https://botanicbynight.com.au/)
📆 Sun 27 Sep, 6:30 PM
📍 Brisbane Botanic Gardens Mt Coot-tha, Brisbane Botanic Gardens Mt Coot-tha, 152 Mt Coot-tha Road, Toowong
💰 See website
`art` `performance` `nature` `wildlife` `indigenous`

Botanic By Night: NightGlow makes its Brisbane debut at Brisbane Botanic Gardens Mt Coot-tha from 25 September to 10 October, transforming the Gardens into a magical after-dark outdoor art experience. Visitors will journey along an enchanting 800-metre route featuring 11 larger-than-life illuminated animal sculptures inspired by Queensland's native wildlife. Designed by Ample Projects in collaboration with First Nations artists Casey Coolwell-Fisher and Nathan Peckham, Botanic By Night: NightGlow combines art, storytelling and sound to celebrate the animals, habitats and stories that shape Queensland's natural environment. From kangaroos and koalas to platypus, dingoes and green tree frogs, each artwork offers a unique encounter, inviting visitors to wander, explore and connect with nature in a whole new way. Perfect for families, friends and date nights, Botanic By Night: NightGlow also features a dedicated food truck precinct.

#### [Teenage Sex and Death at Camp Miasma](https://www.fivestarcinemas.com.au/new-farm/movie/teenage-sex-and-death-at-camp-miasma)
📆 Sun 27 Sep, 9:00 PM
📍 New Farm Cinemas, 701 Brunswick St, New Farm
💰 See link
`film` `performance`

Festival screening. New Farm Cinemas. Direct from winning the Queer Palm at Cannes, Hannah Einbinder and Gillian Anderson electrify in this equally camp and bloody psychosexual thriller from queer maverick Jane Schoenbrun (I Saw the TV Glow). After years of slapdash sequels and waning fandom, the Camp Miasma slasher franchise is handed over to an enthusiastic young director (Einbinder, Hacks) for resurrection. But when she visits the original movie’s star (Anderson, The X-Files), a now-reclusive actress shrouded in mystery, the two women fall into a blood-soaked world of desire, fear, and delirium.

#### [Stories You Wear: Magpie Goose](https://www.museumofbrisbane.com.au/whats-on/stories-you-wear/)
📆 Mon 28 Sep
📍 Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City, Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City Hall, 64 Adelaide Street, Brisbane City
💰 Free
`art` `exhibition` `gallery` `indigenous` `fashion` `culture`

Celebrating Country and Culture through the bold designs of Magpie Goose. Museum of Brisbane is excited to collaborate with Magpie Goose, a proudly Aboriginal owned and led fashion and social enterprise, to celebrate extraordinary First Nations cultural stories and designs. This exclusive to Brisbane exhibition, developed in close collaboration with Magpie Goose owners Amanda Hayman (Wakka Wakka and Kalkadoon) and Troy Casey (Kamilaroi), offers a deep dive into the work of some of the region’s most talented First Nations artists and designers. Using clothing as a canvas for storytelling, this exciting collaboration will connect audiences with the rich and diverse cultural expressions of First Nations communities in Queensland and beyond. Discover the artists and stories behind the brand that’s making a difference, one garment at a time. Stories You Wear: Magpie Goose is supported by the Queensland Government through Arts Queensland. Museum of Brisbane is Brisbane City Council’s leading history and art museum where you can experience our city’s vibrant culture. For more information, visit MoB website. Image: Magpie Goose x Quandamooka Artists, by Amanda Hayman & Troy Casey, Country to Couture, 2025, Photo by Dylan Buckee.

#### [Artist in Residence: Renee Kire](https://www.museumofbrisbane.com.au/whats-on/renee-kire/)
📆 Mon 28 Sep, 10:00 AM
📍 Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City, Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City Hall, 64 Adelaide Street, Brisbane City
💰 Free
`art` `exhibition` `gallery` `performance` `design` `free`

Step inside an exciting new residency project that invites you to experience art in unexpected ways. Artist in Residence Renee Kire takes over Museum of Brisbane’s entryway, transforming these spaces with a vibrant and dynamic mix of striking vinyl graphics and sculptural timber elements. These features create an immersive pathway that stretches across walls and ceilings, guiding you into the heart of the Museum. Drawing inspiration from Minimalism while reinterpreting the movement through a contemporary feminist lens, Kire brings fresh attention to the often-overlooked contributions of women in the field. Bright, colourful and welcoming for all visitors, the artwork is both joyful and thought-provoking. Responding directly to the architecture of the Museum, City Hall and the Brisbane cityscape, Kire’s design reveals unexpected connections between the built environment and abstract form. Repetition, rhythm and thoughtful details invite you to slow down, look closely and discover new perspectives. MoB’s Artist in Residence program is supported by Tim Fairfax AC. Museum of Brisbane is Brisbane City Council’s leading history and art museum where you can experience our city’s vibrant culture.

#### [Lord Mayor’s Photographic Awards 2026](https://www.brisbane.qld.gov.au/events-arts-and-culture/creative-programs-and-awards/lord-mayor-s-photographic-awards)
📆 Mon 28 Sep, 10:00 AM
📍 Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City, Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City Hall, 64 Adelaide Street, Brisbane City
💰 Free
`art` `exhibition` `gallery` `photography` `free`

Brisbane through the lens of local photographers. The winners exhibition of the 2026 Lord Mayor’s Photographic Awards brings together striking photographic works that capture the city’s spirit - from familiar streets and riverside scenes to the fleeting moments, routines and characters that make Brisbane feel like home. New this year, the Brisbane Postcards Collection category invites photographers to showcase Brisbane’s charm and energy through images that locals recognise, and visitors instantly fall in love with. Selected winning works will be available as collectible postcards at MoB Shop. Open to anyone who lives, works or studies in Brisbane, the 2026 Awards celebrate what makes our city unique through this year’s theme, Everyday Brisbane. For more information, visit our website . Museum of Brisbane is Brisbane City Council’s leading history and art museum where you can experience our city’s vibrant culture. For more information, please visit MoB website .

#### [Warrajamba](https://www.museumofbrisbane.com.au/whats-on/warrajamba/)
📆 Mon 28 Sep, 10:00 AM
📍 Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City, Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City Hall, 64 Adelaide Street, Brisbane City
💰 Free
`art` `exhibition` `gallery` `sculpture` `indigenous` `culture`

Follow the mermaid in the bay. Dive into story, culture and hands-on creativity with our latest Artist in Residence, Quandamooka artist Delvene Cockatoo-Collins. Journey alongside Delvene to the shores of Minjerribah and discover the ancestral story of Warrajamba, the mermaid, a living cultural legacy that Delvene continues to explore as she searches for its lost ending. Get hands-on and create your own mermaid or dugong using artist-designed stamps, connecting with the stories of Warrajamba and Yungong, the dugong. Discover Delvene’s powerful sculptural work made from materials gathered from Country - yungair/yungaire (freshwater reed), talwalpin (beach hibiscus cotton tree), and quampie shell. Come and be immersed in a space that celebrates care for land, sea and story. MoB’s Artist in Residence program is supported by Tim Fairfax AC. The Creative Space is proudly supported by Major Partner Brisbane Airport and generously supported by Ian and Cass George, and Holly and John Livingstone. Warrajamba is supported by the Queensland Government through Arts Queensland. Museum of Brisbane is Brisbane City Council’s leading history and art museum where you can experience our city’s vibrant culture.

#### [Fallen Angels (1995)](https://www.fivestarcinemas.com.au/new-farm/movie/fallen-angels)
📆 Mon 28 Sep, 8:30 PM
📍 New Farm Cinemas, 701 Brunswick St, New Farm
💰 See link
`film` `performance`

Classic screening. New Farm Cinemas: Fri 25 September Lost souls reach out for human connection amidst the glimmering night world of Hong Kong in Wong Kar Wai’s hallucinatory, neon-soaked nocturne. This hyper-cool head rush plays like the dark, moody flip side to Wong’s breakout feature 'Chungking Express' as it charts the subtly interlacing fates of a handful of urban loners, including a coolly detached hitman (Leon Lai) looking to go straight, his business partner (Michelle Reis) who secretly yearns for him, and a mute delinquent (Takeshi Kaneshiro) who wreaks mischief by night. Swinging between hardboiled noir and slapstick lunacy with giddy abandon, Fallen Angels is both a dizzying, dazzling city symphony and a poignant meditation on love, loss, and longing in a metropolis that never sleeps.

#### [Passport to the Universe](https://www.trumba.com/calendars/brisbane-city-council?eventid=208417955)
📆 Tue 29 Sep, 12:00 PM
📍 Sir Thomas Brisbane Planetarium, Mt Coot-tha, Sir Thomas Brisbane Planetarium, 152 Mt Coot-tha Road, Mt Coot-tha
💰 Adult $17.50, Child (3-14 yrs) $11, Concession $13.50
`science` `technology`

Get your passport for a thrilling journey through the Solar System, out of the Milky Way Galaxy and to the edge of the observable universe, returning home via a black hole. Narrated by Tom Hanks. Session concludes with a virtual tour of the Brisbane night sky. Passport to the Universe is suitable for ages 8 and over. For younger children, we recommend Perfect Little Planet or Tycho Goes to Mars . Passport to the Universe was developed by the American Museum of Natural History, New York in collaboration with the National Aeronautics and Space Administration (NASA). Major support for new version provided by the California Academy of Sciences, San Francisco.

For younger children, we recommend Perfect Little Planet or Tycho Goes to Mars . #### [Gathering: Muralappi Arts: Jessica Skeen](https://www.trumba.com/calendars/LIVE?eventid=205575135)
📆 Wed 30 Sep, 12:00 PM
📍 Queen Street Mall, Brisbane City, Queen Street Mall, Queen Street, Brisbane City
💰 Free
`art` `performance` `indigenous` `culture` `free`

Gathering presents Muralappi Arts: Jessica Skeen. Jessica Skeen is a contemporary Aboriginal artist and founder of Muralappi Arts, an Indigenous family-owned business with a proud legacy spanning four generations. A Biri and Kuku Thaypan woman from North Queensland, Jessica was raised in Logan and continues to live and work there today. Her artistic practice is grounded in culture, storytelling, and connection to Country, with works that explore themes of healing, identity, and community. Through murals, canvas artworks, and functional art pieces, Jessica creates powerful visual narratives that foster reflection, understanding, and connection. Her work has been featured in hospitals, public spaces, and community initiatives throughout Queensland, enriching shared spaces with stories that celebrate Aboriginal culture and lived experience. Dedicated to sharing culture and knowledge, Jessica also delivers engaging, hands-on workshops that inspire creativity while promoting cultural understanding and respect. Join us on Wednesday 30 September, from 12 noon to 1pm, at the Queen Street Mall Main Stage for a special Muralappi Arts display and showcase presented by Jessica Skeen. This Gathering event is produced by Cre8tive Nations.

Festival screening. New Farm Cinemas. Direct from winning the Queer Palm at Cannes, Hannah Einbinder and Gillian Anderson electrify in this equally camp and bloody psychosexual thriller from queer maverick Jane Schoenbrun (I Saw the TV Glow). After years of slapdash sequels and waning fandom, the Camp Miasma slasher franchise is handed over to an enthusiastic young director (Einbinder, Hacks) for resurrection. #### [Artist in Residence: Renee Kire](https://www.museumofbrisbane.com.au/whats-on/renee-kire/)
📆 Thu 1 Oct, 10:00 AM
📍 Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City, Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City Hall, 64 Adelaide Street, Brisbane City
💰 Free
`art` `exhibition` `gallery` `design` `sculpture` `performance`

Step inside an exciting new residency project that invites you to experience art in unexpected ways. Artist in Residence Renee Kire takes over Museum of Brisbane’s entryway, transforming these spaces with a vibrant and dynamic mix of striking vinyl graphics and sculptural timber elements. These features create an immersive pathway that stretches across walls and ceilings, guiding you into the heart of the Museum. Drawing inspiration from Minimalism while reinterpreting the movement through a contemporary feminist lens, Kire brings fresh attention to the often-overlooked contributions of women in the field. Bright, colourful and welcoming for all visitors, the artwork is both joyful and thought-provoking. Responding directly to the architecture of the Museum, City Hall and the Brisbane cityscape, Kire’s design reveals unexpected connections between the built environment and abstract form. Repetition, rhythm and thoughtful details invite you to slow down, look closely and discover new perspectives. MoB’s Artist in Residence program is supported by Tim Fairfax AC. Museum of Brisbane is Brisbane City Council’s leading history and art museum where you can experience our city’s vibrant culture.

#### [Warrajamba](https://www.museumofbrisbane.com.au/whats-on/warrajamba/)
📆 Thu 1 Oct, 10:00 AM
📍 Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City, Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City Hall, 64 Adelaide Street, Brisbane City
💰 Free
`art` `exhibition` `gallery` `sculpture` `indigenous` `culture`

Follow the mermaid in the bay. Dive into story, culture and hands-on creativity with our latest Artist in Residence, Quandamooka artist Delvene Cockatoo-Collins. Journey alongside Delvene to the shores of Minjerribah and discover the ancestral story of Warrajamba, the mermaid, a living cultural legacy that Delvene continues to explore as she searches for its lost ending. Get hands-on and create your own mermaid or dugong using artist-designed stamps, connecting with the stories of Warrajamba and Yungong, the dugong. Discover Delvene’s powerful sculptural work made from materials gathered from Country - yungair/yungaire (freshwater reed), talwalpin (beach hibiscus cotton tree), and quampie shell. Come and be immersed in a space that celebrates care for land, sea and story. MoB’s Artist in Residence program is supported by Tim Fairfax AC. The Creative Space is proudly supported by Major Partner Brisbane Airport and generously supported by Ian and Cass George, and Holly and John Livingstone. Warrajamba is supported by the Queensland Government through Arts Queensland. Museum of Brisbane is Brisbane City Council’s leading history and art museum where you can experience our city’s vibrant culture.

#### [35mm: Pumpkinhead (1988)](https://www.fivestarcinemas.com.au/new-farm/movie/35mm-pumpkinhead)
📆 Fri 2 Oct, 7:00 PM
📍 New Farm Cinemas, 701 Brunswick St, New Farm
💰 See link
`film` `performance`

35mm Film · Classic screening. New Farm Cinemas. 35mm screening: Fri 2 October. When a group of teenagers inadvertently kill his only son, Ed Harley seeks the powers of a backwoods witch to bring the child back to life. About 35mm: In a world of digital projection and shrinking formats, celluloid is becoming rare — and we think that matters. We hunt down original 35mm prints — often unseen for years — and put them back where they belong: on the big screen. This is cinema with depth, texture, and impact. The way it was meant to be experienced.

#### [Justene Williams: Once We Were Mudskippers](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/justene-williams-once-we-were-mudskippers)
📆 Sat 3 Oct 2026, 10:00 AM – Sun 7 Mar 2027
📍 QAG, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`art` `exhibition` `gallery` `sculpture` `performance` `free`

Spanning performative videos, sculpture, sound and installation from the past 20 years, including a wealth of new works, ‘Once We Were Mudskippers’ emphasises the aesthetic ambition of Justene Williams’s expansive practice. Like the amphibious fish invoked by the exhibition’s title, which exist between worlds, Williams’s artworks are an amalgam of materials and approaches and remain grounded in art history while reflecting contemporary issues. Adopting a do-it-yourself strategy, Williams fuses disparate textures and concepts into compelling artworks that speak to her belief that our shared future can be forged with resources at hand and transformed by the imagination. Williams’s artworks address ideals of beauty, careerist and consumerist pressures, the strangeness of living in both the physical body and virtual space, and human connections to rituals and the afterlife. ‘Once We Were Mudskippers’ is characterised by Justene Williams’s unwavering conviction that art is a world where artists and audiences can experiment and commune.

#### [Dry Leaf](https://www.fivestarcinemas.com.au/new-farm/movie/dry-leaf)
📆 Sat 3 Oct, 12:45 PM
📍 New Farm Cinemas, 701 Brunswick St, New Farm
💰 See link
`film` `performance`

Premiere screening. New Farm Cinemas: Sat 3 October. Presented in partnership with Container: Brisbane Film Society and the Institute of Modern Art (IMA). A Plainwater Films release. QLD PREMIERE. When sports photographer Lisa vanished, she was working on an assignment documenting ramshackle football fields across the country. She has left behind a letter for her parents in Tbilisi instructing them not to follow her – a request that her father, Irakli (played by the filmmaker’s own father, David Koberidze), disregards. He attempts to retrace her cross-country path together with her colleague Levan – who, like many of the characters in this gentle and transfixing saga, happens to be invisible – but theirs is destined to be a sublimely digressive quest, punctuated by curious encounters. Shot entirely on an old Sony Ericsson camera phone and unfolding across an epic timescale, Alexandre Koberidze’s latest feature – winner of both a Competition Special Mention and the FIPRESCI Prize at Locarno Film Festival – combines the sense of melancholic whimsy that distinguished his previous feature, 'What Do We See When We Look at the Sky?', with its daring formal gambit, giving its images of the Georgian countryside a surprising, pixelated beauty at times verging on abstraction.

📆 Sun 4 Oct, 10:00 AM
📍 Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City, Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City Hall, 64 Adelaide Street, Brisbane City
💰 Free
`art` `exhibition` `gallery` `sculpture` `design` `performance`

Step inside an exciting new residency project that invites you to experience art in unexpected ways. Artist in Residence Renee Kire takes over Museum of Brisbane’s entryway, transforming these spaces with a vibrant and dynamic mix of striking vinyl graphics and sculptural timber elements. These features create an immersive pathway that stretches across walls and ceilings, guiding you into the heart of the Museum. Drawing inspiration from Minimalism while reinterpreting the movement through a contemporary feminist lens, Kire brings fresh attention to the often-overlooked contributions of women in the field. Bright, colourful and welcoming for all visitors, the artwork is both joyful and thought-provoking. Responding directly to the architecture of the Museum, City Hall and the Brisbane cityscape, Kire’s design reveals unexpected connections between the built environment and abstract form. Repetition, rhythm and thoughtful details invite you to slow down, look closely and discover new perspectives. MoB’s Artist in Residence program is supported by Tim Fairfax AC. Museum of Brisbane is Brisbane City Council’s leading history and art museum where you can experience our city’s vibrant culture.

#### [Lord Mayor’s Photographic Awards 2026](https://www.brisbane.qld.gov.au/events-arts-and-culture/creative-programs-and-awards/lord-mayor-s-photographic-awards)
📆 Sun 4 Oct, 10:00 AM
📍 Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City, Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City Hall, 64 Adelaide Street, Brisbane City
💰 Free
`art` `exhibition` `gallery` `photography` `free`

Brisbane through the lens of local photographers. The winners exhibition of the 2026 Lord Mayor’s Photographic Awards brings together striking photographic works that capture the city’s spirit - from familiar streets and riverside scenes to the fleeting moments, routines and characters that make Brisbane feel like home. New this year, the Brisbane Postcards Collection category invites photographers to showcase Brisbane’s charm and energy through images that locals recognise, and visitors instantly fall in love with. Selected winning works will be available as collectible postcards at MoB Shop. Open to anyone who lives, works or studies in Brisbane, the 2026 Awards celebrate what makes our city unique through this year’s theme, Everyday Brisbane. For more information, visit our website . Museum of Brisbane is Brisbane City Council’s leading history and art museum where you can experience our city’s vibrant culture. For more information, please visit MoB website .

#### [Warrajamba](https://www.museumofbrisbane.com.au/whats-on/warrajamba/)
📆 Sun 4 Oct, 10:00 AM
📍 Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City, Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City Hall, 64 Adelaide Street, Brisbane City
💰 Free
`art` `exhibition` `gallery` `indigenous` `sculpture` `performance`

Follow the mermaid in the bay. Dive into story, culture and hands-on creativity with our latest Artist in Residence, Quandamooka artist Delvene Cockatoo-Collins. Journey alongside Delvene to the shores of Minjerribah and discover the ancestral story of Warrajamba, the mermaid, a living cultural legacy that Delvene continues to explore as she searches for its lost ending. Get hands-on and create your own mermaid or dugong using artist-designed stamps, connecting with the stories of Warrajamba and Yungong, the dugong. Discover Delvene’s powerful sculptural work made from materials gathered from Country - yungair/yungaire (freshwater reed), talwalpin (beach hibiscus cotton tree), and quampie shell. Come and be immersed in a space that celebrates care for land, sea and story. MoB’s Artist in Residence program is supported by Tim Fairfax AC. The Creative Space is proudly supported by Major Partner Brisbane Airport and generously supported by Ian and Cass George, and Holly and John Livingstone. Warrajamba is supported by the Queensland Government through Arts Queensland. Museum of Brisbane is Brisbane City Council’s leading history and art museum where you can experience our city’s vibrant culture.

#### [Botanic By Night: NightGlow](https://botanicbynight.com.au/)
📆 Sun 4 Oct, 6:30 PM
📍 Brisbane Botanic Gardens Mt Coot-tha, Brisbane Botanic Gardens Mt Coot-tha, 152 Mt Coot-tha Road, Toowong
💰 See website
`art` `exhibition` `sculpture` `nature` `wildlife` `indigenous`

Botanic By Night: NightGlow makes its Brisbane debut at Brisbane Botanic Gardens Mt Coot-tha from 25 September to 10 October, transforming the Gardens into a magical after-dark outdoor art experience. Visitors will journey along an enchanting 800-metre route featuring 11 larger-than-life illuminated animal sculptures inspired by Queensland's native wildlife. Designed by Ample Projects in collaboration with First Nations artists Casey Coolwell-Fisher and Nathan Peckham, Botanic By Night: NightGlow combines art, storytelling and sound to celebrate the animals, habitats and stories that shape Queensland's natural environment. From kangaroos and koalas to platypus, dingoes and green tree frogs, each artwork offers a unique encounter, inviting visitors to wander, explore and connect with nature in a whole new way. Perfect for families, friends and date nights, Botanic By Night: NightGlow also features a dedicated food truck precinct.

#### [Small Figures](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/small-figures)
📆 Wed 20 Sep 2023, 10:00 AM – Tue 26 Jan 2027
📍 QAG, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`art` `collection` `whimsical` `digital` `free`

The Collection Online team have been busy photographing thousands of artworks as part of QAGOMA’s Digital Transformation Initiative. This makes the Collection available and accessible to people everywhere, in detail never seen before. With dedicated conservators, registrars, technologists and photographers, the project has provided the resources to delve deep into Collection Storage to uncover and care for artworks not displayed in decades. This exhibition represents a small sample of our teams’ favourite finds – the weird and whimsical, the cute and curious. We gratefully acknowledge the hundreds of donors who generously supported the QAGOMA Foundation’s ‘Unlock the Collection’ campaign to digitise the Collection and make artworks more accessible and discoverable online including this diverse range of artworks, from small figures to small frogs. These are some of the artworks we have recently prepared for photography, and just some of the works which made us smile.

Highlights 📜 The largest immersive expedition of its kind dedicated to ancient Egypt 🧭 Travel back of 4500 years in time to the heart of ancient Egypt 🏆 Over 2.5+ million visitors worldwide 😎 Move freely through the Great Pyramid of Giza in a fully immersive virtual reality experience 🔎 Enter forbidden corridors, uncover the mysteries of the Queen’s Chamber, and meet the goddess Bastet 👨‍👩‍👧‍👧 Enjoy an intimate and educational experience designed especially for small groups 🌟 Developed in collaboration with Egyptologist Peter der Manuelian from Harvard University 🎁 Gift memories to someone special! Purchase a gift card here General Info 📅 Date: from March 2026 ⏳ Duration: approx. 1 hour, including 45 minutes of immersion walking 📍 Location: Fever Immersive Center - Level 1, 123 Gotha St, Fortitude Valley QLD 👤 Age requirement: 10+ with a minimum height of 1.30m (anyone aged 10-15 must be accompanied by an adult; anyone between 15-17 attending without an adult must bring a pre-signed waiver form) ♿ Accessibility: the venue is wheelchair accessible, but electric wheelchairs must switch to manual mode or alternatively use our manual wheelchairs onsite ❓ Please consult the FAQs of this experience here Description Put on your virtual reality headset and fasten your seatbelt! In Horizon of Khufu , travel thousands of years back to the heart of ancient Egypt. Discover the secrets of one of the seven wonders of the world through an innovative concept: the immersive expedition. Explore the Great Pyramid of Giza as it was more than 4500 years ago. Walk through its mysterious corridors, enter the Queen’s Chamber, and even try to meet the goddess Bastet, in a unique experience in Brisbane. Ready to discover how people lived during Egypt’s 4th Dynasty? Get your tickets now for Horizon of Khufu: Journey to Ancient Egypt in Brisbane!

#### [Capturing Beauty – Solo show by Carol Oh](https://rqas.com.au/event/carol-oh-2026-show/)
📆 Wed 23 Sep – Sun 27 Sep
📍 Petrie Terrace Gallery, 3/162 Petrie Terrace
💰 See link
`art` `exhibition` `painting`

[vc_row css=”.vc_custom_1523319263370{padding-top: 20px !important;padding-bottom: 30px !important;}”][vc_column][vc_separator color=”custom” border_width=”10″ accent_color=”#4ba686″][vc_column_text]Brisbane-based artist Carol Oh creates works in oil paint and pen that celebrate the timeless beauty of the human form, nature, architecture, and the quiet poetry of everyday life. Her diverse body of work encompasses exquisite floral studies, portraiture, figurative subjects, landscapes, and finely detailed pen drawings of Rome and its enduring architectural splendour. Drawing and painting from a young age, Carol has cultivated a lifelong passion for capturing beauty, atmosphere, and character. Her work reflects a deep appreciation for both the grandeur and subtlety found in the world around us. This exhibition, Capturing Beauty, brings together a collection of portraiture, figurative, floral, and landscape works. Through her art, Carol invites viewers into moments of reflection and connection, creating evocative imagery that lingers in the memory and resonates beyond the canvas. She continues to expand her artistic practice and welcomes commissioned work. Exhibition will be on show at RQAS Brisbane Gallery from Wednesday, 23rd of September to Sunday, 27th of September.[/vc_column_text][vc_separator color=”custom” border_width=”10″ accent_color=”#4ba686″][vc_single_image image=”26108″ img_size=”full”][/vc_column][/vc_row]

#### [Our Communities Textiles Treasures : Textiles Exhibition](https://redlandscoastmuseum.org.au/exhibitions/our-communities-textiles-treasures-textiles-exhibition/)
📆 Thu 1 Oct 2026 – Sun 31 Jan 2027
📍 Redlands Coast Museum
💰 See link
`art` `exhibition` `history` `textiles` `craft` `culture`

Every textile has a story to tell, and every story helps preserve the history of the Redlands. Community Textile Treasures celebrates a collection of special textile items connected to local families, memories, and milestones. From handmade garments and embroidered linens to treasured keepsakes and heirloom pieces, each item on display carries a unique story waiting to be shared. Among the exhibition are pieces lovingly created for family members, treasured sewing projects, inherited textiles, and items that have become cherished reminders of people, places, and moments in time. Together, they create a rich tapestry of community history, craftsmanship, and personal connections. As you explore the exhibition, discover the fascinating stories woven into every stitch and learn how these treasured objects help tell the story of the Redlands and the people who have called it home.

#### [Audrey Gibbs Art Prize](https://rqas.com.au/event/audrey-gibbs-art-prize/)
📆 Fri 2 Oct, 10:00 AM
📍 Petrie Terrace Gallery, Petrie Terrace, Petrie Terrace Gallery, 162 Petrie Terrace, Petrie Terrace
💰 Free
`art` `exhibition` `painting` `free`

Audrey Gibbs joined the Royal Queensland Art Society in 1983 and was recognised as a Fellow of the society in 2009. This exhibition explores genre artworks, one of Audrey’s great pleasures. This exhibition focuses on Genre Artworks, one of Audrey's favourite themes to paint. Genre Artworks are those that depict ordinary people in everyday situations, both in the exterior and the interior. Rather than heroic or mythological subjects, these works celebrate the familiar: everyday people participating in domestic routines, work, leisure, social gatherings, and quiet moments of daily life. The Royal Queensland Art Society is proudly supported by Brisbane City Council.

#### [Audrey Gibbs Art Prize](https://rqas.com.au/event/audrey-gibbs-art-prize/)
📆 Sun 4 Oct, 10:00 AM
📍 Petrie Terrace Gallery, Petrie Terrace, Petrie Terrace Gallery, 162 Petrie Terrace, Petrie Terrace
💰 Free
`art` `exhibition` `painting` `free`

Audrey Gibbs joined the Royal Queensland Art Society in 1983 and was recognised as a Fellow of the society in 2009. This exhibition explores genre artworks, one of Audrey’s great pleasures. This exhibition focuses on Genre Artworks, one of Audrey's favourite themes to paint. Genre Artworks are those that depict ordinary people in everyday situations, both in the exterior and the interior. Rather than heroic or mythological subjects, these works celebrate the familiar: everyday people participating in domestic routines, work, leisure, social gatherings, and quiet moments of daily life. The Royal Queensland Art Society is proudly supported by Brisbane City Council.

You'll never look at a cardboard box the same way again! Prepare to have your minds blown! Four quirky misfits turn everyday junk – wheelie bins, kitchen scraps, plastic bags – into an electrifying symphony of beats, rhythm and creativity. This high-energy adventure transforms trash into treasure with explosive drumming, dance and laugh-out-loud comedy. An abandoned kitchen becomes a drum set, plastic bags turn into instruments and the human body becomes a sound machine! It's a thrilling celebration of rhythm, imagination and sustainability for all ages. Speaking in 'Rhythmish' – a playful language made of rhythm and gibberish – this show will hook you from start to finish! Join these musical misfits who reinvigorate forgotten junk. Tickets for Birthday Celebrations at Dinos Alive 🎫 Birthday ticket - includes entry to the experience and birthday room for the celebration 🎫 VIP Birthday ticket - includes entry to the experience and birthday room for the celebration, virtual reality and a Dinos Alive poster * Please always select the 'Birthday Room' option when booking so that we can ensure that the room is reserved exclusively for your celebration. ** A minimum of 7 tickets required for a birthday booking. Highlights 🌲 Travel back to the Jurassic in a thematic park full of realistic dinosaurs—see these magnificent creatures in their natural habitat! 🦕 Come face to face with 40+ specimens of moving replicas: T-rex, Stegosaurus, Ankylosaurus, Velociraptors, and many more 🦈 Dive into the depths of the seas and interact with Jurassic sea giants in a unique virtual aquarium 😮 Learn more about the Triassic, Jurassic and Cretaceous periods in a fun and interactive way 👓 Immerse yourself to the fullest with a captivating VR experience (for an additional fee) 👨‍👩‍👧‍👦 Kids can enjoy a “Budding Experts” interactive space, where they can become palaeontologists and tame the most iconic dinosaurs General Info 📅 Dates and times: select your date & time directly in the ticket selector ⏳ Duration: approx. 60-90 minutes. You will have the room booked for you for 30 minutes 📍 Location: Fever Skygate | Exhibition and Experience Centre, 30-34 The Circuit, Skygate, Brisbane Airport 👤 Age requirement: all ages are welcome! ♿ Accessibility: the venue is wheelchair accessible ❓ Please consult the FAQs of this experience here ❓ Please contact us at dinos.brisbane@exhibitionhub.com if you have any questions Birthday Celebrations ⏳ You will have access to the Birthday Room for 30 minutes 🎂 2 soft drinks per guest are included in the birthday package 👥 Please note that any person entering the exhibition or the birthday room needs a valid ticket 👥 If you are unsure about the number of attendees, please select the minimum; you can add more attendees upon arrival ⚠ Tickets are not refundable Description Go back millions of years to a land ruled by giants! This immersive experience in Brisbane brings you face-to-face with the fearsome and spectacular dinosaurs that dominated the animal world for 140 million years. From the turkey-sized Velociraptors to the mighty T-Rex, interact with life-sized, moving animatronics of a large variety of species, and see these magnificent creatures in their natural Jurassic habitat. Step into another world and learn something new in an experience that's fun for the whole family! Get your tickets now to celebrate your birthday with us!

#block-yui_3_17_2_1_1787275646529_9720 { --opacity: 100%; --translate-x: 0px; --translate-y: 0px; --rotation: 0deg; --scale-x: 100%; --scale-y: 100%; --skew-x: 0deg; --skew-y: 0deg; --origin-x: 50%; --origin-y: 50%; --block-transform: none; opacity: var(--opacity, 1); transform: var(--block-transform); transform-origin: var(--origin-x) var(--origin-y); } Join multiple Archibald Portrait Prize finalist David Fenoglio for a two-day portrait painting workshop. The workshop will focus on natural and realistic representation of the live model in oils. Limited to 8 places. 9am - 3:30pm both days. An alla prima approach will be demonstrated, including blocking in shapes, proportion, anatomy, design, value/colour relationships, colour mixing and paint handling. Foundational principles will be discussed while demonstrating a variety of technical approaches. Day one will focus on anatomy and proportion with students creating a block-in drawing and one short alla prima painting from the live model. Day two will focus on modelling form, colour mixing and paint application with students creating one longer alla prima painting from a live model. #block-cc2ba05bcd2c9bbadd9e { } #block-cc2ba05bcd2c9bbadd9e .sqs-html-content { } #block-cc2ba05bcd2c9bbadd9e { mix-blend-mode: var(--tweak-text-block-blend ); border-radius: var(--tweak-text-block-radius); } @media screen and (max-width: 767px) { #block-cc2ba05bcd2c9bbadd9e { } } @media screen and (max-width: 767px) { #block-cc2ba05bcd2c9bbadd9e .sqs-html-content { } } @media screen and (max-width: 767px) { } #block-yui_3_17_2_1_1787275646529_11920 { --opacity: 100%; --translate-x: 0px; --translate-y: 0px; --rotation: 0deg; --scale-x: 100%; --scale-y: 100%; --skew-x: 0deg; --skew-y: 0deg; --origin-x: 50%; --origin-y: 50%; --block-transform: none; opacity: var(--opacity, 1); transform: var(--block-transform); transform-origin: var(--origin-x) var(--origin-y); } LEARN MORE #block-yui_3_17_2_1_1787275646529_12294 { --sqs-block-content-flex: 0; } #block-yui_3_17_2_1_1787275646529_12294 { --opacity: 100%; --translate-x: 0px; --translate-y: 0px; --rotation: 0deg; --scale-x: 100%; --scale-y: 100%; --skew-x: 0deg; --skew-y: 0deg; --origin-x: 50%; --origin-y: 50%; --block-transform: none; opacity: var(--opacity, 1); transform: var(--block-transform); transform-origin: var(--origin-x) var(--origin-y); }

#### [Mindful Art Mornings](https://museumofbrisbane.t.qtix.com.au/events/019eaeb1-4087-f11b-0c69-cda5244fec29)
📆 Wed 30 Sep, 7:30 AM
📍 Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City, Museum of Brisbane, Brisbane City Hall, 64 Adelaide Street, Brisbane City
💰 Free
`art` `wellness` `meditation` `free`

Start your day with intention at Museum of Brisbane. Mindful Art Morning invites early risers to the Museum before opening hours for a gentle, guided moment of pause. Within the calm of the gallery, this rotating program offers an artist-led mindful experience designed to help you reset, reconnect and ease into the day ahead. After the session, linger over a light morning tea, with space for quiet conversation and reflection. MoB is more than a museum - it is a place for the people, a portal to our past, and a mirror to contemporary life. Make Mindful Art Mornings your restorative midweek ritual in the heart of the city. For individual session details, please visit the MoB website. Presented by Museum of Brisbane in association with Brisbane Festival. Museum of Brisbane is Brisbane City Council’s leading history and art museum, where you can experience our city’s vibrant culture.

[vc_row][vc_column][vc_column_text] DETAILS [/vc_column_text][vc_separator color=”custom” border_width=”5″ el_width=”10″ accent_color=”#d35847″][vc_column_text] These untutored sessions follow a classical life drawing class structure of short and longer poses by a nude life model, your medium is your choice in these classes, watercolours, acrylics, graphite, charcoal – bring along whatever materials you enjoy working with and get your hand flowing with the practice of capturing form. Easels, drawing donkey, & boards supplied, artists to supply all other materials. #### [School holiday activity - Spring hub - Flower power](https://www.eventbrite.com.au/e/school-holiday-activity-spring-hub-flower-power-tickets-1997533521535)
📆 Thu 24 Sep, 10:00 AM
📍 Brisbane Botanic Gardens Mt Coot-tha, Brisbane Botanic Gardens Mt Coot-tha, 152 Mt Coot-tha Road, Toowong
💰 Free
`science` `art` `nature` `kids` `family` `free`

Discover the fascinating world of flowers and the peculiar Pollinators who work with them at our Spring hub school holiday activities at the Brisbane Botanic Gardens Mt Coot-tha. Take off on a scientific exploration of our Sensory Garden, bursting with blooms of all shapes and sizes and bring your findings back to the hub where you can take a closer look with microscopes and magnifying glasses. Get your hands dirty and turn science into art by making 3D clay pollen models and sensational seed balls. Finally compile all your field notes and decorate them with pen or a paintbrush while you discover more about the precious pollinators who help to keep our flowers fantastic and our fruit plentiful. Bring a picnic blanket and lunch to enjoy on our grassy lawn. Note: Children under 16 are to be accompanied by an adult. Location : Meet at the Auditorium lawn. Please wear a hat and enclosed shoes; and bring a keep cup or water bottle. This workshop supports the Botanic Gardens' commitment to creating a plant loving community dedicated to putting the plant back into planet. For more information, send an email .

#### [School holiday activity - Spring hub - Flower power](https://www.eventbrite.com.au/e/school-holiday-activity-spring-hub-flower-power-tickets-1997533754231)
📆 Tue 29 Sep, 10:00 AM
📍 Brisbane Botanic Gardens Mt Coot-tha, Brisbane Botanic Gardens Mt Coot-tha, 152 Mt Coot-tha Road, Toowong
💰 Free
`kids` `family` `science` `nature` `art` `craft`

Discover the fascinating world of flowers and the peculiar Pollinators who work with them at our Spring hub school holiday activities at the Brisbane Botanic Gardens Mt Coot-tha. Take off on a scientific exploration of our Sensory Garden, bursting with blooms of all shapes and sizes and bring your findings back to the hub where you can take a closer look with microscopes and magnifying glasses. Get your hands dirty and turn science into art by making 3D clay pollen models and sensational seed balls. Finally compile all your field notes and decorate them with pen or a paintbrush while you discover more about the precious pollinators who help to keep our flowers fantastic and our fruit plentiful. Bring a picnic blanket and lunch to enjoy on our grassy lawn. Note: Children under 16 are to be accompanied by an adult. Location : Meet at the Auditorium lawn. Please wear a hat and enclosed shoes; and bring a keep cup or water bottle. This workshop supports the Botanic Gardens' commitment to creating a plant loving community dedicated to putting the plant back into planet. For more information, send an email .
